| 4 | weeks, C++ | Runtime cutting: Smash Hit's plane-splitting of convex compounds on saturated contact impulses with five randomised planes and at most three re-solves per step; Siege's 2D polygon clipping of planar walls; NVIDIA Blast (BSD-3, physics-agnostic) as a module; Jolt's `MutableCompoundShape` add/remove sub-shapes as the primitive (script exposure unchecked); CoACD (MIT) for fewer, collision-aware hulls than V-HACD | ([Gustafsson GDC 2015 PDF](https://archive.org/download/GDC2015Gustafsson/GDC2015-Gustafsson.pdf); [Voxagon, Cracking destruction](https://blog.voxagon.se/2014/05/13/cracking-destruction.html); [Jolt ReleaseNotes.md](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jrouwe/JoltPhysics/master/Docs/ReleaseNotes.md); [CoACD](https://github.com/SarahWeiii/CoACD)) |
| 5 | months, C++ | Voxels: per-object volumes, deterministic cut commands ("cut hole in this shape at voxel coord x,y,z", "change ownership", "reconnect joint"), per-island bodies; Teardown's structural-integrity prototype is attributed but unverified ("one building at 60 fps", X post unreadable); `godot_voxel` gives editable terrain, not per-island bodies | ([Voxagon, Teardown multiplayer](https://blog.voxagon.se/2026/03/13/teardown-multiplayer.html); [80.lv on the Teardown prototype](https://80.lv/articles/this-real-time-voxel-building-demolition-is-so-satisfying); [Zylann/godot_voxel](https://github.com/Zylann/godot_voxel)) |
| 6 | months, C++ or compute, research skills | DMM-class finite elements, next | — |

Tier three is where round one lives because it is where the strength ladder's thresholds live, and two 2018–2025 results endorse choosing it over FEM: Energized Rigid Body Fracture banks the elastic energy stored in a breakable constraint and releases it as kinetic energy on failure, giving "results comparable to continuum mechanics approaches, but with the computational efficiency of rigid body simulation", which is a few lines in a bond-graph implementation and answers Parker & O'Brien's "deadened motions" objection ([Li, Andrews, Jones, Bargteil 2018, record](https://api.semanticscholar.org/graph/v1/paper/DOI:10.1145/3203207)); and Augmented Vertex Block Descent's own brick-wall test is a rigid-body wall "attached via breakable hard constraints with a maximum force threshold" in which sequential impulse and XPBD fail to hold the constraints and VBD "bends, but does not break", so the current best solver's showcase for destruction is the tier-three model with a stiffer solver ([Giles, Diaz, Yuksel 2025, AVBD PDF](https://graphics.cs.utah.edu/research/projects/avbd/Augmented_VBD-SIGGRAPH25.pdf)). Battlefield 6 (2025) ships "part-based destruction and debris spawning systems", health transitions and networked "prioritization and culling of part destruction", and an outside observer describes predetermined model states swapped in under smoke, the same family ([EA, How Battlefield 6 redefined destruction](https://www.ea.com/news/how-battlefield-6-redefined-destruction); [Escapist on Battlefield 6](https://www.escapistmagazine.com/battlefield-6-destruction-physics/)). Godot's guidance applies at every tier: primitives and few convex hulls on dynamic bodies, no trimesh, continuous CD for fast bodies, sleeping on ([Godot docs, Troubleshooting physics](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/latest/tutorials/physics/troubleshooting_physics_issues.html)).

### The DMM verdict

Digital Molecular Matter is real, published and console-proven, but proprietary middleware whose public footprint stops at a Maya 2019 plugin. Pixelux (Geneva; founded 2003/2004; CTO Eric Parker) developed it "6 years in conjunction with LucasArts", its timeline ends in 2012, its DMM Engine page still says "available for evaluation and licensing", the plugin is $499 per seat with a free version capped at 2,500 elements, the 2015 Academy honour was a **Technical Achievement Award (Academy Certificate)** to Parker, O'Brien and Cole, the product announcement dates from 18 September 2008 (a search snippet claiming 2023 is wrong), and O'Brien's own page now says he "was Chief Scientist at Pixelux" ([Pixelux Company](https://www.pixelux.com/company.html); [Pixelux DMM Engine](https://www.pixelux.com/DMMengine.html); [Pixelux DMM Plugin](https://www.pixelux.com/DMMplugin.php); [Oscars Sci-Tech 2015](https://www.oscars.org/sci-tech/ceremonies/2015); [Game Developer, Pixelux announcement 2008](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/game-platforms/pixelux-announces-digital-molecular-matter-products)). The first edition's list of shipped titles is corrected: Remedy licensed the **DMM Playback System**, in which authoring-tool simulations "play back in real-time during gameplay with GPU acceleration" as vertex animation in Northlight, used mainly for "cars, doors, walkways, metal beams, bridges, chains, cargo containers", while Remedy's engine page credits "A physics library built on PhysX" for runtime destruction; so Quantum Break is an authoring/playback title and the last shipped *runtime* DMM game is The Force Unleashed II (2010), which strengthens the "assume no active runtime SDK" conclusion ([Pixelux press release 2013](https://www.pixelux.com/Remedy-QuantumBreak2013.html); [fxguide on Quantum Break](https://www.fxguide.com/fxfeatured/time-for-destruction-the-tech-of-quantum-break/); [Remedy, Northlight](https://www.remedygames.com/northlight)). The Force Unleashed's three-engine coupling had a documented failure mode, a Euphoria stormtrooper thrown at a DMM wall "with such velocity that the game engine couldn't make the calculation fast enough, so the stormtrooper would sail right through the wall" ([Computer Graphics World, Sept 2008](https://www.cgw.com/Publications/CGW/2008/Volume-31-Issue-9-Sept-2008-/Force-To-Be-Reckoned-With.aspx)).

The method is fully described: a corotational tetrahedral FEM with linearised backward Euler, lumped mass and Rayleigh damping, `(M + Δt C + Δt² K) v⁺ = Δt f + M v − Δt K (x − u)` solved by conjugate gradient to a relative error of 0.001 with a designer-set iteration cap, fracture by per-node separation tensor with node replication when the largest eigenvalue exceeds the material toughness, tet splitting disabled, no fracture below three face-connected tets, artist "splinters", sleeping islands, and a coarse tet cage skinning the render mesh (a 256-bone limit motivated texture skinning); the disclosed cost is the glass scene of about **2,400 tetrahedra** on a 2.4 GHz Q6600, whose single-processor curve peaks near 21 ms at the initial fracture and then runs 5–10 ms, and 3–8 ms on four processors (read from the plots), console timings withheld by contract ([Parker & O'Brien 2009 PDF](http://graphics.berkeley.edu/papers/Parker-RTD-2009-08/Parker-RTD-2009-08.pdf)). Its ancestors are open: O'Brien & Hodgins' brittle fracture, its ductile extension ("only a simple to implement modification that is computationally inexpensive"), and Müller & Gross's warped-stiffness FEM at a 0.01 s implicit step, "fast enough to animate a few thousand volumetric elements at interactive rates" on a 1.8 GHz Pentium 4 ([O'Brien, Bargteil, Hodgins 2002 PDF](http://graphics.berkeley.edu/papers/Obrien-GMA-2002-08/Obrien-GMA-2002-08.pdf); [Müller & Gross 2004 PDF](https://matthias-research.github.io/pages/publications/GI2004.pdf)). So "proprietary or reproducible" is both: the algorithm is reproducible from the paper and its citations, and no open-source library does real-time FEM *fracture*. Vega FEM (BSD-3) has corotational and nonlinear materials with implicit integrators and no fracture; PhysX 5's FEM soft bodies are GPU-only and leave "tearing/cutting" to the application; Bullet has `cutLink` and `refine(…, cut)` but no stress fracture; Jolt's soft bodies have no tearing or vertex-splitting API in any release through 5.6.0 (an inference from absence, not a quoted statement; the "fairly expensive to simulate" line in Jolt's docs is about dynamic mesh shapes, not soft bodies), and Godot's wrapper never populates Jolt's tetrahedral volume constraints (`jolt_soft_body_3d.cpp:142-143, 223`) ([VegaFEM mirror](https://github.com/starseeker/VegaFEM); [PhysX 5.3.1 Soft Bodies](https://nvidia-omniverse.github.io/PhysX/physx/5.3.1/docs/SoftBodies.html); [btSoftBody.h](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bulletphysics/bullet3/master/src/BulletSoftBody/btSoftBody.h); [Jolt ReleaseNotes.md](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jrouwe/JoltPhysics/master/Docs/ReleaseNotes.md)). Vertex Block Descent and its augmented variant change the *cost*, not the recommendation: an unconditionally stable, GPU-parallel elastic solver at a fixed 1/60 s that tolerates element deletion and vertex duplication is now published with Apache-2.0 reference code (Newton's VBD solvers on Warp), but it is a separate GPU solver whose coupling to the Jolt character is a second engineering problem, and the crack criterion, tet meshing (fTetWild MPL-2.0 or TetGen AGPL), splinters and island management remain to be written ([Chen et al. 2024, VBD PDF](https://graphics.cs.utah.edu/research/projects/vbd/vbd-siggraph2024.pdf); [Newton README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/newton-physics/newton/main/README.md); [fTetWild README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wildmeshing/fTetWild/master/README.md); [WIAS TetGen page](https://wias-berlin.de/software/index.jsp?id=TetGen&lang=1)). The FEM tier therefore stays "months, C++ or compute, only if the wood-and-steel bending fantasy is essential", with lower research risk than the first edition implied; its one unique property, stored elastic energy released at fracture, is faked for hero objects with the Energized-RBF impulse, a plastic-deformation shader or a short soft-body swap. TFU tuned its fantasy by editing physical parameters rather than scripts ([fxguide, DMM: FEA for VFX](https://www.fxguide.com/fxfeatured/dmm-fea-for-vfx/)).

### Determinism and replay (design requirement)

The first edition said both that the seeded wobble is "fully reproducible" and that determinism "is not guaranteed by Godot's Jolt integration". The honest statement: Jolt "runs deterministically" and its unreleased notes remove `PhysicsSettings::mDeterministicSimulation` because "The simulation is now always deterministic", with an ARM64/x64 double-precision determinism fix listed under 5.6.0 on the release page and under "unreleased" in `ReleaseNotes.md`; the official Godot Jolt page is silent on determinism; the "not able to make such guarantees" caveat is the deprecated godot-jolt *extension's* README; the residual risk lives in Godot's node and server layer (call ordering, threads, `run_on_separate_thread` "should be considered experimental") ([Jolt README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jrouwe/JoltPhysics/master/README.md); [Jolt ReleaseNotes.md](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jrouwe/JoltPhysics/master/Docs/ReleaseNotes.md); [Godot docs, Using Jolt Physics](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/latest/tutorials/physics/using_jolt_physics.html); [godot-jolt README](https://github.com/godot-jolt/godot-jolt)). Replay is defined as **input replay with periodic state snapshots**: seeds for the jitter and the ring-buffer delay are part of the input stream, the pelvis trajectory is diffed against the recording in CI, and any divergence is located in the Godot layer, not in Jolt. Destruction records an **event log** (Teardown's deterministic command stream, about 1 Mbit per client; Siege, Teardown and Crackdown all replicate events, not meshes), which is also the save format; Teardown's own engine "isn't deterministic" as a whole and runs a hybrid of deterministic destruction commands plus state sync, which is the model here ([Voxagon, Teardown multiplayer](https://blog.voxagon.se/2026/03/13/teardown-multiplayer.html)). The identical-input replay test is the first Phase 0b item; it has not yet been run.

### A per-frame budget against the fork's ray-tracing renderer (design requirement)

The headless build excluded `servers/rendering/renderer_rd`, where the fork's ray-tracing work lives, so nothing about rendering was measured, and no note holds a budget. The fork's own commit history sets the stakes: idle skinned actors "cost 4.3 ms of builds a frame on the bridge" of the TPS demo before skinned BLAS rebuilds were gated on the skinning dispatch count (commit `2e7bd81b6`), and batching the frame's deformed-BLAS refits took "~0.85 ms off the frame" with 21 skinned BLASes live (commit `10317bc6e`). A ragdoll changes pose every tick, so it refits its BLAS every frame; destruction churns instances, which rebuilds the TLAS; debris pools are `MultiMesh`, which the red team's reading of `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/raytracing_scene.cpp:786-801` says the scene traces along with particles. The requirement is a measurement on the "TPS bridge" scene named in those commits, with a 15-body ragdoll at 60 / 120 / 240 Hz plus 50–500 awake shards and a 2,048-instance `MultiMesh`, reporting physics, BLAS refit/build and TLAS time separately, before Milestone 4 fixes chunk counts. Jolt's own multicore scaling data (a 3,680-body ragdoll scene, "16 piles of 10 ragdolls … with motors active", 4.9× on 8 threads, 5.7× with SMT on 16, scaling stopping around 16 cores) is the only physics-scaling anchor, and the Phase 0 benchmark's finding that one thread beats four at fifteen bodies says the character and the debris must be profiled together ([Rouwé, Jolt Physics Multicore Scaling](https://jrouwe.nl/jolt/JoltPhysicsMulticoreScaling.pdf)). A Godot + Jolt "bodies per millisecond" figure still does not exist.

### Impact audio and haptics (design requirement)

The legibility argument in the first edition was visual only; sound and rumble are the cheapest consequence channel and every reference ships them. Overgrowth tiers body-fall sounds by contact impulse (> 1 light, > 2 medium, > 3 heavy, > 4 "bodyfall") and spawns skid particles above 1 ([aschar.as](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WolfireGames/overgrowth/main/Data/Scripts/aschar.as)); Source plays landing sounds at volume 0.5 / 0.85 / 1.0 by fall speed and splits the rumble into `RUMBLE_FALL_LONG` when the volume exceeds 0.85 ([gamemovement.cpp](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ValveSoftware/source-sdk-2013/master/src/game/shared/gamemovement.cpp)); Siege notes that "Destruction changes the acoustic of the environment drastically" and feeds destruction into sound propagation ([L'Heureux, GDC 2016 PDF](https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2016/Presentations/LHeureux_Julien_Art_Of_Destruction.pdf)); Battlefield 6 keys "seam and surface emitters" to debris activation and shockwaves by visual material ([EA, How Battlefield 6 redefined destruction](https://www.ea.com/news/how-battlefield-6-redefined-destruction)). The rule: the same per-tick Δv and joint-lambda tiers that drive the damage model drive a body-impact sound set (three or four tiers per material) and controller rumble, with the long/short split at the tier where injury begins, so a player hears the difference between a tier-safe and a tier-lethal landing before the health bar moves.

### Persistence of destruction (design requirement)

Nothing in the first edition survived a save or a reload. The event log above is the answer: replaying the deterministic command stream rebuilds the bond graph's state, which is why the graph must be defined in terms of commands (break bond i, detach island j) rather than mesh snapshots, a constraint on Milestone 4's data model; Battlefield 6's "We wanted the result of destruction to stay, creating interesting ruins and level-designed debris piles" is the design intent, and Megaton Rainfall's persistence across unload is undocumented and appears to reset with missions ([EA, How Battlefield 6 redefined destruction](https://www.ea.com/news/how-battlefield-6-redefined-destruction)).

## Megaton Rainfall's scale lessons: capabilities and engineering only

### What del Cerro actually said

The developer's own words now anchor the engine choice and the two headline capabilities. He wrote a custom engine because Unreal "was not cheap for Indies" in January 2012, he disliked Unity, and "The common engines start asking the size for the terrain: 512x512? 1024x1024? But I want to make the terrain of the entire Earth, that is a sphere of radius 6370 kilometers! Where is the check box to do that?"; "Both things (the real scale of the Earth and the supersonic speed) have never been done in a First Person game, although we have more than enough technology to achieve it (but it's an engineering nightmare)"; "you will fly at Mach 8"; "in half a minute go from the day side of the Earth to the night side" (about 670 km/s exo-atmospheric, this report's arithmetic); the building-collapse technology "looked so promising" in December 2013 that he went full-time; cities and terrain are procedural; the solar systems reuse the planet renderer ([Cobra TV GNN interview, Dec 2015](http://www.cobratvgnn.com/2015/12/megaton-rainfall-developer-interview.html)). The Steam description promises to "break the sound barrier… circumnavigate the Earth in seconds" but names no Mach figure; "Mach 8 in 5 seconds" is Wikipedia quoting the 2015 IGF entry (unopened here) and "Mach 10" is an unverified IndieDB snippet ([Steam](https://store.steampowered.com/app/430210/Megaton_Rainfall/); [Megaton Rainfall, Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megaton_Rainfall)). Flight speed is "adjusted automatically depending on a number of factors" (never named) and flying "freely through a setting of astronomical proportions" was "the most difficult power to implement" ([AP2HYC interview](https://ap2hyc.com/2015/04/interview-alfonso-del-cerro-lead-developer-of-megaton-rainfall/)). The destruction pipeline is "a proprietary solution including CPU-based physics simulation, GPU-accelerated particles and render target tricks", the render-target trick being the snapshot: buildings are a hidden "paper" mesh and a displayed "inflated" mesh, an explosion removes paper triangles near the blast and re-inflates, and continuity comes from "a snapshot of the house just as it's seen from the camera. Yes, a snapshot. A bitmap" used as the texture of GPU billboard particles ("The PS4 can handle bazillions easily"), chosen over pre-fractured variants for memory at "200 squared miles" of city ([Wccftech interview 2016](https://wccftech.com/megaton-rainfall-interview-real-sized-earth-save/); [PlayStation Blog, The Tech That Powers Megaton Rainfall](https://blog.playstation.com/2017/10/17/the-tech-that-powers-megaton-rainfall-launching-today-on-ps-vr/); [PlayStation Blog EU](https://blog.playstation.com/archive/2017/10/17/how-procedurally-destructable-cities-work-in-ps-vr-superhero-sim-megaton-rainfall-out-today/)). The game "is super-GPU bound. We use a lot of instancing, a lot of arrays of textures in shaders to minimize the number render commands, so the render thread is several milliseconds idle every frame already", scales by "density of city stuff and the render distance", and is CPU-limited only on PSVR, which reprojects 60 fps to 120 ([Wccftech, 9 Mar 2016](https://wccftech.com/megaton-rainfall-targeting-1080p60fps-medium-settings-ps4-release-q3q4/)). What reviewers report about the *feel* of flight is the design datum: "flying ice skates", overshoot, "pop-in", mid-flight streaming hitches ("cargas a mitad de un vuelo"), and a fixed peripheral blur "only fully rendering the world in a very large circle at the center of the screen" that is not eye-tracked foveation ([UploadVR review](https://www.uploadvr.com/megaton-rainfall-review-google-earth-meets-man-steel/); [VidaExtra review](https://www.vidaextra.com/analisis/analisis-de-megaton-rainfall-los-matamarcianos-arcade-en-la-epoca-de-la-realidad-virtual)). Comfort was handled by control choice and, it turns out, by platform requirement: the peripheral "blinders" were Eagle-Flight-style black frames that "apparently Sony requires" and initially could not be disabled, with a launch option to minimise them; snap turns of 45° are the default for the most sensitive players; the VR build removed the visible body because a straight body while seated "wasn't convincing"; the six VR interfaces are enumerated nowhere ([Real o Virtual, Gamescom 2017](https://www.realovirtual.com/articulos/5027/actualizado-megaton-rainfall-impresiones-entrevista-gamescom-2017); [Real o Virtual, 2015](https://www.realovirtual.com/articulos/27/megaton-rainfall-madrid-games-week); [Vandal review](https://vandal.elespanol.com/analisis/ps4/megaton-rainfall/34431); [Palmieri, Eagle Flight R&D, VRDC 2016](https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1023922/Full-Speed-Flying-in-VR)). A launch bug tied gameplay speed to display refresh with vsync, for which Godot's fixed tick plus interpolation is the standing answer ([Steam announcements](https://steamcommunity.com/app/430210/allnews/)). No postmortem, talk or precision-scheme statement by del Cerro exists in anything reachable.

### Godot's precision stack and Jolt's velocity envelope

Godot's large-world page gives the single-precision envelope as an on-foot area "not exceeding 8192×8192 meters" where "precision remains acceptable within that range, even for a first-person game" (the first edition's "double remains acceptable beyond 262,144 units" misread a comparison row), names origin shifting as the low-end alternative it declines to detail, and documents `precision=double` builds (".double" suffix, custom export templates, GDExtension ABI change, a performance penalty on 32-bit CPUs) with emulated-double rendering from [PR #66178](https://github.com/godotengine/godot/pull/66178) (the lost precision computed on the CPU and passed to the GPU for an error-corrected vertex position; `MODEL_MATRIX` read-only; not with `skip_vertex_transform` or `world_vertex_coords`; particles and multimeshes handled separately; degrading beyond roughly 10,000 km) ([Godot docs, Large world coordinates](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/latest/tutorials/physics/large_world_coordinates.html)). Reverse-Z landed in 4.3 as "a massive improvement to depth buffer precision at no performance or memory cost" ([Introducing Reverse Z, Godot blog](https://godotengine.org/article/introducing-reverse-z/)); far-distance rendering issues are tracked in [issue #98655](https://github.com/godotengine/godot/issues/98655) (directional shadows in large worlds, #81877, still open), and `Camera3D.far` defaults to 4,000 m (`doc/classes/Camera3D.xml:183`). An origin rebase is project code: [proposal #15350](https://github.com/godotengine/godot-proposals/issues/15350) for `SceneTree.rebase_world_origin()` was closed as not planned, and [proposal #15436](https://github.com/godotengine/godot-proposals/issues/15436) lists what already teleports cleanly (`Node3D.global_position`, bodies via `PhysicsServer3D.body_set_state`, render instances via `RenderingServer.instance_teleport`) and what does not: "there is currently no way to teleport a `GPUParticles3D` or `Trail3D`", which is not cosmetic here because Megaton's whole destruction look is GPU billboard particles. The fork compiles Jolt with `JPH_DOUBLE_PRECISION` under `precision=double` and crosses positions as `JPH::RVec3` (`modules/jolt_physics/SCsub:156-157`; `misc/jolt_type_conversions.h:134-146`); Terrain3D's double-precision support is "experimental and has had only one user give a positive report so far" ([Terrain3D double precision docs](https://terrain3d.readthedocs.io/en/stable/docs/double_precision.html)).

Jolt's own documentation adds the constraint the first edition missed. Single precision is "accurate enough" within roughly 5 km of the origin; the double build makes positions accurate "even at thousands of kilometers" at a 5–10 % cost; but "The broadphase uses floats internally", "Rotations (Quat) are tracked in floats", and "Jolt stores velocities in floats, so the large velocities that are common in space will become inaccurate. This will especially be visible if you create an object with constraints (e.g. a ragdoll) and make it move at high speeds. The relative velocities between the bodies will be too low for a float to represent accurately, which means that the constraints will not be solved properly"; the recommended pattern, as in X4: Foundations, is to "split the universe into multiple PhysicsSystems and keep objects in each PhysicsSystem close to the origin and with low velocities. E.g.: A ship that is traveling near light speed can happen in a PhysicsSystem that is traveling at near light speed"; the recommended envelope is bodies of 0.1–10 m, speeds of 0–500 m/s and statics of 0.1–2,000 m ([Jolt Physics documentation](https://jrouwe.github.io/JoltPhysics/index.html)). Mach 8 at 343 m/s is about 2,744 m/s, 5.5× the default `max_linear_velocity` and 5.5× Jolt's envelope. So the first edition's Milestone 6 instruction "raise `limits/max_linear_velocity`", cited to a docs page that never mentions the setting, becomes: the flying body lives in a **co-moving physics space** whose bulk velocity is subtracted each tick (the Krakensbane pattern), the world moves past it (a floating origin, per-tile local space in Outerra's manner), and the linear and angular caps and the 2,000 m world boundary are raised only for unconstrained debris. Godot's multi-space API exists (`PhysicsServer3D.space_create`, `World3D.space`) and object hand-off between spaces is manual project code (inference). This is the shipped architecture in every planet-scale first-person system found: Star Citizen moved "physics and positioning" to 64-bit with camera-relative rendering, a zone system and "local physics grids", at a cost Sean Tracy called "not better performance … If anything, it's a bit worse – but we're talking marginal differences" ([RSI, Monthly Report September 2014](https://robertsspaceindustries.com/en/comm-link/transmission/14191-Monthly-Report-September-2014); [RSI, Monthly Studio Report June 2015](https://robertsspaceindustries.com/en/comm-link/transmission/14814-Monthly-Studio-Report-June-2015); [GamersNexus, Sean Tracy on 64-bit](https://gamersnexus.net/gg/2622-star-citizen-sean-tracy-64bit-engine-tech-edge-blending)); Kerbal Space Program runs a floating origin and Krakensbane as two separate "astro-scale physics helpers" whose engagement thresholds "were quite high; high enough that they were not engaging enough and were allowing errors to creep in" until 1.2 lowered them ([Squad devnote, Sept 2016](https://kerbaldevteam.tumblr.com/post/150703936094/devnote-tuesday-12-pre-release-the-bug-hunt-and)); Elite Dangerous needs "millimeter precision" from noise inputs of "tens of billions of millimeters", so Frontier wrote 64-bit and dual-float noise libraries and spherifies cube-face quadtree patches in compute shaders ([80.lv, Generating the Universe in Elite Dangerous](https://80.lv/articles/generating-the-universe-in-elite-dangerous)); Outerra generates each terrain tile in its own local space with the camera expressed in that space and uses a logarithmic depth buffer ([Outerra, Intro 2008](https://outerra.blogspot.com/2008/07/intro.html); [Outerra, Floating Point Depth Buffer](https://outerra.blogspot.com/2009/12/floating-point-depth-buffer.html)); UE5's Large World Coordinates split CPU doubles from GPU DoubleFloat emulation with Niagara particles as the known weak point, still Beta ([Large World Coordinates in UE5](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/large-world-coordinates-in-unreal-engine-5)); and Outer Wilds is small (Brittle Hollow radius ≈ 308 m, Timber Hearth orbit ≈ 8.6 km by enthusiast measurement; Wikipedia says only "exaggerated system of Newtonian physics", not "shrank") ([The Physics Mill on Outer Wilds](https://www.thephysicsmill.com/2024/09/20/an-astrophysicist-attempts-to-measure-the-physics-of-outer-wilds/); [Outer Wilds, Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outer_Wilds)). Planet-scale terrain LOD is published and engine-agnostic: geometry clipmaps (nested viewer-centred grids, fractal refinement, 60 fps flight over a 40 GB US height map), P-BDAM (which solved single-precision accuracy on a planet-sized mesh with per-patch GPU local frames), chunked LOD, and GPU tessellation of spherical height fields ([Losasso & Hoppe 2004 PDF](https://hhoppe.com/geomclipmap.pdf); [Cignoni et al. 2003, P-BDAM PDF](http://www.crs4.it/vic/data/papers/ieeeviz03-pbdam.pdf); [Ulrich, Chunked LOD](http://tulrich.com/geekstuff/chunklod.html)); all of it is project code in Godot (`ArrayMesh`, compute shaders), with far shadows, particle precision and streaming as the parts the engine does not supply.

### Speed regimes and comfort at speed

Extreme speed is split into regimes in every reference: Megaton's supersonic, superluminal and dash tiers (wiki-only), Elite's supercruise from "29.9 kilometers-per-second to 2001c" scaled by distance from gravity wells ("The closer you are to the planet, the greater the effect"; wiki-only) and its separate hyperspace ([Elite Dangerous Wiki, Supercruise](https://elite-dangerous.fandom.com/wiki/Supercruise)). The engineering content is that each regime boundary is also a *frame* boundary: switch the body's physics space and subtract bulk velocity once speeds leave Jolt's 500 m/s envelope, and key the max-speed curve to distance from the nearest surface, which is trivially GDScript. The comfort layer has a peer-reviewed basis and touches no engine code: dynamic FOV restriction (Fernandes & Feiner; Wu & Suma Rosenberg's optical-flow-driven and asymmetric-lateral variants; Ubisoft's "dynamic peripheral vision blinders" for Eagle Flight) ([Wu & Suma Rosenberg 2022](https://doi.org/10.1145/3562939.3565611)), distance-graded haze to raise perceived speed, snap or quarter turns, and So, Lo & Ho's finding that nausea plateaus above about 10 m/s so the accelerations, not the top speed, need the care. Criterion's advice for arcade speed feel ("a solid physical simulation with a whole lot of goodness on top") and Superflight's stick curve ("Small nudges of the stick will result in precise and tiny movements, but there's a point on the pad where that gives way to large, sweeping turns") are the tuning references ([Harris, Vehicle Feel Masterclass, GDC 2018](https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1025295/Vehicle-Feel-Masterclass-Balancing-Arcade); [Kotaku on Superflight](https://kotaku.com/superflight-is-a-fast-game-about-dying-a-lot-1820280557)). Del Cerro's order of operations, destruction rendering first (2012–2013), flight and planet transitions next, comfort last, remains a reasonable template: the expensive systems were prototyped before the game existed.

## Houdini: offline authoring through glTF, with Houdini 22 as the version gate

### Houdini Engine for Godot exists, and the licence still decides its role

SideFX's official Houdini Engine hosts are Unreal, Unity, Maya, 3ds Max and a Maxon-maintained Cinema 4D plug-in; Godot appears on none of the product, FAQ or Labs pages; the FAQ states that custom HAPI plug-ins "require a commercial Houdini Engine license" (Workstation $525 per year, floating from $795), and the HAPI licensing documentation gives the check order (Engine → Core → FX → Engine Indie → Indie), states that "Indie license usage is limited to authorized plugins (Unity, Unreal, Maya, Max, C4D)", that "Non-commercial licenses (ie. Apprentice) are not supported", and that "tainting" a session by mixing licence tiers "is not allowed within Houdini Engine" ([Houdini Engine FAQs](https://www.sidefx.com/faq/houdini-engine-faq/); [Houdini Engine plug-ins](https://www.sidefx.com/products/houdini-engine/plug-ins/); [HAPI Licensing](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/hengine/_h_a_p_i__licensing.html)). Houdini Indie costs $299 for one year or $449 for two, is capped at $100K revenue, saves in its own `.hiplc`/`.hdalc` formats, and does allow third-party renderers inside Houdini proper; the restriction is specific to Engine hosts ([Houdini Indie](https://www.sidefx.com/products/houdini-indie/); [Indie licensing, SideFX Support](https://www.sidefx.com/Support/licensing/indie/)). **HEGo** by Peter Prickarz is the one integration: a C++ GDExtension for Godot 4.4+ and Houdini 22.0.429+ (v0.7.0, 2026-09-15, Linux and Windows x86_64; releases from v0.1.1 on 2025-02-17), MIT-licensed, 159 stars and 220 commits, with a GDScript API for sessions, HDA nodes, geometry, curve and heightfield inputs, asynchronous cooking and a Terrain3D bridge; its README reads verbatim "No license needed for building, active commercial(FX,Core or Houdini Engine) license required for runtime" (the first edition's smoothed sentence was not a quotation), the SideFX forum thread has eight posts and no SideFX staff, and the author's own statement of 31 March 2026 is that "Indie doesn't allow third party plugins outside of the official ones" ([HEGo README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/peterprickarz/hego/main/README.md); [HEGo releases](https://github.com/peterprickarz/hego/releases); [HEGo Getting Started](https://hego.readthedocs.io/en/latest/about/getting_started.html); [SideFX forum, Houdini Engine for Godot Release](https://www.sidefx.com/forum/topic/101121/)). One first-edition claim is withdrawn: HEGo v0.7.0 states that "exported projects load HEGo at last", so "shipped builds could never depend on HEGo" is technically false; the argument is now licensing only, because HAPI checks out an Engine, Core, FX, Engine Indie or Indie licence on the running machine. For an indie-budget team the route stays offline export on a Houdini Indie seat, with HEGo as an editor-time tool only on a commercial seat. The older houdini2godot exporter (TSCN/ESCN plus glTF, Houdini 20, MIT, last commit 2025-04-08) is unverified on 4.4+ ([Mandrake0/houdini2godot](https://github.com/Mandrake0/houdini2godot)).

### The glTF contract needs Houdini 22, or a sidecar

The offline contract is glTF with metadata, and it is version-gated. The features the first edition attributed to "Houdini's generic glTF ROP", Build Hierarchy from Path Attribute, Export Extras (dictionary attributes `gltf_scene_extras`, `gltf_node_extras`, `gltf_mesh_extras`, `gltf_skin_extras` at detail or primitive class), Export Custom Attributes (prefixed `_`, point class), Draco and single/multiple clip export, exist only in the **Houdini 22.0** ROP ("Since 22.0"); the Houdini 21.0 ROP has 46 parameters and none of them; Houdini 22 shipped on 2026-06-23 ([glTF ROP, Houdini 22.0 docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/out/gltf.html); [glTF ROP, Houdini 21.0 docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini21.0/nodes/out/gltf.html); [What's new in Houdini 22: engine](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/news/22/engine.html); [Digital Production, Here comes Houdini 22](https://digitalproduction.com/2026/06/23/here-comes-houdini-22/)). Neither ROP writes skins; skinned characters go through the KineFX **ROP GLTF Character Output** (Houdini 19+), which exports skin from `boneCapture`, joints, clips and morph targets but has **no extras or custom-attribute option** in its parameter list ([ROP GLTF Character Output docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/kinefx--rop_gltfcharacteroutput.html)). Three routes carry ragdoll metadata on a character: a JSON sidecar written from hython, a Post-Write Script that opens the emitted `.gltf` and injects `extras` onto joint nodes, or name-encoding on joints. On the Godot side, glTF `extras` land in node, mesh and material metadata since 4.4 ([godot PR #86183](https://github.com/godotengine/godot/pull/86183)), and, new in the second pass, extras on joint nodes survive skeleton collapse as **bone metadata** (`modules/gltf/skin_tool.cpp:640-642` → `Skeleton3D.set_bone_meta(bone, "extras", …)`), so the Post-Write route yields `Skeleton3D.get_bone_meta(i, "extras")` directly. Draco stays off, cited to the fork's own `supported_extensions` list (`GODOT_single_root`, `KHR_animation_pointer`, `KHR_lights_punctual`, `KHR_materials_emissive_strength`, `KHR_materials_pbrSpecularGlossiness`, `KHR_materials_unlit`, `KHR_node_visibility`, `KHR_texture_transform`, plus registered `KHR_texture_basisu`, `EXT_texture_webp`, `OMI_collider`, `OMI_physics_body`, `OMI_physics_shape`; `modules/gltf/gltf_document.cpp:7011-7023`), not to the closed and archived [issue #73738](https://github.com/godotengine/godot/issues/73738). `EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing` is still not imported ([godot-proposals #8787](https://github.com/godotengine/godot-proposals/issues/8787); [godot issue #109280](https://github.com/godotengine/godot/issues/109280)), so scatter ships as points or a sidecar and is instanced on import. Node-name suffixes (`-col`, `-convcol`, `-colonly`, `-convcolonly`, `-rigid`, `-navmesh`, `-occ`, `-noimp`, `-loop`, separators `-`, `$`, `_`) apply, and `-convcolonly` = V-HACD is confirmed by the local importer (`editor/import/3d/resource_importer_scene.cpp:480`: "otherwise VHACD will be used"), not by the docs page ([Godot docs, node type customization](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/assets_pipeline/importing_3d_scenes/node_type_customization.html)). Blender reaches the same metadata with a checkbox ("Custom Properties — Export custom properties as glTF extras") and must keep "GPU Instances" and Draco off for Godot ([Blender 4.5 manual, glTF 2.0](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/4.5/addons/import_export/scene_gltf2.html)).

### Destruction authoring: the constraint graph is Houdini's decisive advantage

**RBD Material Fracture** (the "Since 22.0" version) produces per-material pieces (Concrete, Glass, Wood, Custom, and Metal, whose bending and tearing are new in Houdini 22), a constraint network as two-point polylines between piece centroids with `name`, `constraint_tag`, `creaseweights` and per-level fracture ids, and proxy geometry by Voronoi, convex decomposition or packed spheres; **RBD Constraint Properties** writes glue (`strength`, −1 = unbreakable, `half_life`, `propagation_rate`, `propagate_iteration`), hard (`cfm`, `erp`, angular motors) and soft (`stiffness`, `damping_ratio`) attributes, now paintable on the source geometry; the cone-twist, slider and spring types the first edition listed were not re-surfaced this pass ([RBD Material Fracture docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/rbdmaterialfracture.html); [What's new in Houdini 22: RBD](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/news/22/rbd.html); [RBD Constraint Properties docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/rbdconstraintproperties.html)). Glue `strength` has **no documented units**: it is compared against an accumulated, decaying, network-propagated collision impulse in the Bullet solver ("Whenever a glued object gets hit, it accumulates a glue impulse force"; propagation "will not travel more than this number of bonds each solve step"), so only ordering and ratios are portable and the Godot runtime recalibrates it against its own impulses and reimplements decay and propagation if it wants the Houdini look ([Glue Constraint Relationship DOP docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/dop/glueconrel.html); [Houdini destruction docs, constraint networks](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/destruction/constraints.html)). That graph is a bond graph already: nodes are pieces by `name`, edges are constraint primitives, weight is `strength`; serialise it as glTF extras on the destructible's root (Houdini 22) or from hython, export per-piece hulls from the Convex Decomposition SOP as `<piece>-convcolonly` children, and use the RBD Bullet Solver only to tune strengths offline or to bake cinematic collapses. Two shipped pipelines confirm the shape: all destructible assets in The Finals "are pre-fractured into smaller pieces in Houdini, with seams carefully hidden until the asset is damaged", because "Real-time fracturing is generally too computationally expensive and unpredictable for a fast-paced multiplayer game", with collision hulls from "a custom 2D convex decomposition algorithm written in VEX" ([SideFX, Making the Procedural Buildings of THE FINALS](https://www.sidefx.com/community/making-the-procedural-buildings-of-the-finals-using-houdini/)); and Houdini's Chaos integration carries pieces to Unreal as `unreal_gc_piece` cluster levels, per-piece implicit shapes, mass flags and damage thresholds, a ready template for the Godot extras schema whose one missing element, the explicit connection graph, is exactly what Houdini's polylines add ([Houdini Engine for Unreal, Geometry Collections](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/unreal/chaosintegration.html)). Godot's importer is extensible for this: `GLTFDocumentExtension` runs GDScript at twelve import and export stages (stateless, via `GLTFState`/`GLTFNode.set_additional_data`), the fork natively imports `OMI_collider`, `OMI_physics_body` and `OMI_physics_shape` (shape types box, capsule, cylinder, sphere, hull, trimesh; body types static, animatable, character, rigid, vehicle, trigger), so a Post-Write Script can inject `OMI_physics_shape`/`OMI_physics_body` JSON and get `RigidBody3D` plus `CollisionShape3D` per piece without name suffixes, and the OMI Group's MIT Godot add-on implements `OMI_physics_joint` (limits and drives, no break force) as the nearest existing "generic JSON constraint importer" ([GLTFDocumentExtension class reference](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/latest/classes/class_gltfdocumentextension.html); [OMI_physics_body README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/omigroup/gltf-extensions/main/extensions/2.0/OMI_physics_body/README.md); [OMI_physics_joint README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/omigroup/gltf-extensions/main/extensions/2.0/OMI_physics_joint/README.md); [omigroup/omi-godot](https://github.com/omigroup/omi-godot)). A second baked-destruction path besides VAT exists: **Labs RBD to FBX 2.0** turns packed-primitive transforms into bones, and Godot's ufbx importer supports skins and rigging, so a Houdini Bullet collapse plays back as an ordinary skeletal `Animation` (untested here) ([Labs RBD to FBX 2.0 docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/out/labs--rbd_to_fbx-2.0.html)). Labs Vertex Animation Textures 3.0 targets Unreal and Unity only; the MIT Godot port ships a `.hdalc` HDA (Labs VAT 3.1, limited-commercial format, which would taint a commercial seat), requires uncompressed textures and glTF export, and does not name EXR ([Labs VAT 3.0 docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/out/labs--vertex_animation_textures-3.0.html); [Godot_VAT3 README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/G4ND44/Godot_VAT3/main/README.md)). PDG's Wedge TOP batch-exports strength and seed variants in one cook ([Wedge TOP docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/top/wedge.html)).

### Ragdoll, terrain and the FEM that is not there

For the ragdoll, KineFX's **Configure Joints** writes a dictionary point attribute whose keys are `rotation_lower_limits` and `rotation_upper_limits` in radians relative to the rest transform, `rotation_order` (which assigns twist to the first axis and up to the second in Ragdoll mode), `rest_xform`, `rotation_weights`, `mass` and `local_com`; the first edition's "twist/up axes stored in the dictionary" is imprecise, they are implied by the rotation order, so a post-import script derives cone span (upper − lower on the two non-twist axes) and twist span and can use `mass` and `local_com` for `PhysicalBone3D.mass` and shape offsets ([solvephysfbik VEX docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/vex/functions/solvephysfbik.html); [Configure Joints docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/kinefx--configurejoints.html)). **Ragdoll Collision Shapes** generates convex hulls or basic shapes per joint from skin weights as packed primitives with `name` (with a "Display Intersections" check because intersections "typically cause solver instability"), and **Ragdoll Solver 2.0** is a wrapper around a Bullet DOP network ("Bullet World Scale"), so its stiffness and damping ratios do not transfer to Jolt; Houdini 22's ragdoll tool adds an "Impact Threshold parameter [that] enables objects to become ragdoll when struck", an offline way to author hit-reaction thresholds the Godot controller mirrors ([Ragdoll Collision Shapes docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/kinefx--ragdollcollisionshapes.html); [Ragdoll Solver 2.0 docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/kinefx--ragdollsolver.html); [What's new in Houdini 22: KineFX](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/news/22/kinefx.html)). The mapping to Godot's `PhysicalBone3D` joint enum (NONE 0, PIN 1, CONE 2, HINGE 3, SLIDER 4, 6DOF 5) stands (`doc/classes/PhysicalBone3D.xml:126-141`). For terrain, HeightField Output writes 16- and 32-bit float EXR ([HeightField Output docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/heightfield_output.html)); Terrain3D 1.0.2 "Supports Godot 4.4-4.6 and possibly later versions", imports 16/32-bit EXR ("Only use 16 or 32-bit height data"), and on 4.8-dev must be built from source against godot-cpp v10 (its PR #1044, open, planned for Terrain3D 1.2), which HEGo's Terrain3D bridge inherits ([Terrain3D import docs](https://terrain3d.readthedocs.io/en/stable/docs/import_export.html); [Terrain3D installation docs](https://terrain3d.readthedocs.io/en/stable/docs/installation.html); [Terrain3D PR #1044](https://github.com/TokisanGames/Terrain3D/pull/1044)); HTerrain's code accepts PNG, EXR, RAW and XYZ (no HDR, no R16), its author does "only … bug fixes these days", and neither plugin is verified on 4.8-dev ([HTerrain README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Zylann/godot_heightmap_plugin/master/README.md)). Should the team ever pursue FEM, no Godot tetrahedral loader or soft-body-from-tets GDExtension exists; Jolt has tetrahedral volume constraints (`SoftBodySharedSettings.h:207, 351`) that Godot's wrapper never populates, so the path is Tet Embed → hython → TetGen `.node`/`.ele` into a custom module ([Tet Embed docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/tetembed.html); [TetGen file formats](https://wias-berlin.de/software/tetgen/fformats.html)). Blender reaches Godot equally well for modelling and skinning and Cell Fracture produces pieces, but the authored constraint graph, per-piece proxies and strength values would have to be scripted in Blender Python, which is why Houdini earns its seat on this game's destruction ([glTF-Blender-IO](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO)).

## Learned control stays a later residual, and its cost argument is re-based

The runtime interface is unchanged: a policy queried at 20–50 Hz (30 Hz in DeepMimic, AMP, ASE, CALM, PHC, PULSE, MaskedMimic, PARC, Motivo; 50 Hz in AMOR and the 2026 robotics stack) outputs PD targets for a ragdoll simulated at 60–1,200 Hz, and inference is cheap (DReCon's 2×128 tanh network costs 60 µs of a 340 µs single-threaded frame; SuperTrack's 5×1024 ELU network about 1.8 ms with 20 MB of weights over 240 Hz physics) ([DReCon PDF](https://static-wordpress.ubisoft.com/montreal.ubisoft.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/11/13214229/DReCon.pdf); [SuperTrack PDF](https://theorangeduck.com/media/uploads/other_stuff/SuperTrack.pdf); [AMOR, arXiv 2505.23708](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.23708)). Four statements in the first edition are corrected against the primaries. DeepMimic and AMP simulate in **Bullet at 1.2 kHz**, Won 2022 in PyBullet and ControlVAE in ODE, so "only DReCon and SuperTrack were ever trained in a game-class engine" is false; the defensible claim is that DReCon and SuperTrack are the only ones *engineered to game frame budgets* (60–240 Hz solvers, motor constraints, ≤ 1.8 ms inference) ([DeepMimic PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1804.02717); [AMP PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2104.02180); [facebookresearch/PhysicsVAE](https://github.com/facebookresearch/PhysicsVAE); [ControlVAE PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.06063)). CALM only *anticipates* that its "robustness envelope may limit its ability … such as climbing stairs or walking on uneven terrain"; it reports no such failure ([CALM PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.02195)). "The native ONNX GDExtension is unmaintained since February 2024" is true of joemarshall's extension and stale as a conclusion: mat490's loader was touched in June 2026, DynamicDevices' refreshed fork (Godot 4.6+, ONNX Runtime 1.20.1, C++, MIT, float32 tensors of rank 0–8) has commits from 27 August to 1 September 2026, godot-native-rl runs ncnn statically linked for Godot 4.5+ on Linux, macOS, Web, Windows, Android and iOS, and godot-infer runs ONNX on compute shaders inside Godot's rendering pipeline; none publishes a latency number, and the stock Godot RL Agents ONNX path still needs the .NET build ([DynamicDevices/godot-onnx-loader](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DynamicDevices/godot-onnx-loader/main/README.md); [minigraphx/godot-native-rl](https://github.com/minigraphx/godot-native-rl); [FrancoYudica/godot-infer](https://github.com/FrancoYudica/godot-infer); [godot_rl_agents README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/edbeeching/godot_rl_agents/main/README.md)). And the simulator gap for a DReCon-sized residual is about **one order of magnitude, not two to three**: Isaac Gym's "2-3 orders of magnitude" claim is relative to CPU-simulator RL pipelines in general, while the end-to-end sample rates of the controllers this report cites were 10²–10⁴ per second (DeepMimic ≈ 350, AMP 600–900, DReCon 2,500, SuperTrack ≈ 5,000, ASE ≈ 11,600), against Godot RL Agents' "12k interactions per second on a high end laptop computer, when parallized on 4 CPU cores" ([Isaac Gym, arXiv](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.10470); [Godot RL Agents paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2112.03636)).

Training cost has collapsed at the low end and grown at the high end. DeepMimic-style skills now train in **1.5–4.5 s each** on one RTX 5090 with MuJoCo Warp, and a 37.4 h AMASS tracker reaches 98.6 % success in 29.1 minutes ([InstantMimic, arXiv 2609.09821](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.09821)); humanoid locomotion with pushes and rough terrain trains in 15 minutes on one RTX 4090 ([Seo et al., arXiv 2512.01996](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.01996)); whole-body tracking in about 45 minutes ([LooperMuscle, arXiv 2608.00820](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.00820)); a protective-fall and get-up policy in about 5 hours ([Xu et al., arXiv 2511.07407](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.07407)); PDP's diffusion policies in 1.5 hours per 15-motion model on a 2080 Ti ([PDP, arXiv 2406.00960](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.00960)). At the other end sit GPC's 24 A100s, SONIC's 21k GPU-hours (now Science Robotics 11(117), 2026-08-12), HIL's four V100s for parkour vaulting, PhysicsFC's thirty days on an A6000, and the unchanged mid-range (ASE ten days on a V100, MaskedMimic two weeks on four A100s, PARC a month) ([GPC, arXiv 2606.29148](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.29148); [SONIC, Science Robotics](https://doi.org/10.1126/scirobotics.aed4592); [HIL, arXiv 2505.12619](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.12619); [ASE PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.01906); [MaskedMimic PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.14393); [PARC PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.04002)). So the obstacle is no longer compute but the **Jolt transfer step**: no published work trains or evaluates a character controller in Jolt; a PhysX-trained low-level tracker moved to MuJoCo but the hierarchical AMP policy on top "falls in ~0.9–2 s for every velocity command", and PolySim needed three simulators to gain 52.8 % success in a fourth ([IsaacLab issue #7750](https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab/issues/7750); [PolySim](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.01708)). Two 2025–2026 papers map directly onto this design: AMOR conditions one policy on reward weights so style and robustness are dialled at runtime "without requiring retraining", a learned analogue of the competence scalar at five days of training per character ([AMOR, arXiv 2505.23708](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.23708)); Neural Assistive Impulses learns a non-physical root wrench whose allowed magnitude is the knob and penalises its use, so a standard rigid-body engine reproduces "physics-defying" moves, and notes the raw impulse "exceeding 4kN at 60Hz, and 8kN at 120Hz", which is the pelvis authority spring with a citation ([Neural Assistive Impulses, arXiv 2604.05394](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.05394)). Single trackers that recover from falls without a separate get-up policy now exist (StableMimic, Stubborn), softening the first edition's "tracker or ragdoll" dichotomy ([StableMimic, arXiv 2608.02385](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02385); [Stubborn, arXiv 2606.12814](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.12814)).

The one shipped title is still ARC Raiders, and its talks now give the production recipe. Machine learning is "strictly for locomotion", behaviour trees decide intent, five to ten people worked "for years", and it "was almost cut multiple times because it didn't meet our quality bar" ([80.lv, ARC Raiders ML interview](https://80.lv/articles/inside-the-magic-of-machine-learning-that-powers-enemy-ai-in-arc-raiders)). From the GDC 2026 and NDC26 talks as reported by 4Gamer: actions were **target angles for PD controllers**; PPO and SAC; early tests in MuJoCo, Isaac Gym and a Unity test bed, deployment in Unreal; a network trained to both walk and stop became timid ("behavior bleed"), so the fix was **one independent policy per task, switched by the game engine's behaviour tree**; reward lists grew to "10–15 reward models" that "do not scale" (an art note like "more bouncy" cost weeks to months of re-tuning); style came from adversarial imitation of an animator-authored walk cycle; perception is egocentric point-cloud raycasts through PointNet after a height-map failed in tunnels; a sibling project applied a penalised "magic force" to the head when the robot was about to fall; finishing one robot's walk took "years"; and the Leaper's failed landings "make the robot look real" ([4Gamer GDC 2026 report](https://www.4gamer.net/games/609/G060942/20260312064/); [4Gamer NDC26 report](https://www.4gamer.net/games/652/G065211/20260616016/)). ADD's adversarial differential discriminator removes exactly the reward-weight tuning Embark says consumed the years, with Sony PlayStation researchers as co-authors ([ADD, arXiv 2505.04961](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.04961)). SuperTrack's authors' caution stands: RL's "unpredictability of the algorithm's success can make it a risky choice in a production setting" ([SuperTrack PDF](https://theorangeduck.com/media/uploads/other_stuff/SuperTrack.pdf)).

The recommendation holds. Hand-author the PD base; keep Learning to Get Up's torque-quality finding and the 3CC fatigue model as design tools usable without learning ([Cheema et al. 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.08583)); use DeepMimic's push protocol, DReCon's projectiles at 3 m/s and SuperTrack's cubes as acceptance tests; keep the ledge grab an explicit constraint (PARC's ledge catching emerged only after a month of GPU time). If learning is ever added, the smallest-risk shape is a DReCon-sized 2×128 residual on the hand-authored PD targets, trained either in a headless Jolt harness (a few thousand samples per second is the DReCon/SuperTrack regime, not hopeless) or in MuJoCo Warp with domain randomisation over gains, friction and contact and a transfer step that the PolySim and issue #7750 evidence says works for a single low-level tracker and not for a hierarchy; reserved for NPC bodies before the player body; and run through a hand-written MLP forward pass or ncnn for a CPU-side policy on the physics tick, with godot-onnx-loader as a viable but week-old iteration path and godot-infer the wrong tool because it is GPU-bound.

## The phased plan: Phase 0 is delivered, and every later phase has exit criteria, not durations

The first edition attached "(days)", "(weeks)" and "(months)" to its milestones; no note estimates effort for an active-ragdoll controller, a competence opening or a camera stack, and where the notes speak to controller feel they point the other way: Dying Light's movement took "1.5 years" of iteration after the prototype, Mirror's Edge ran "2 Week cycles, 2-3 moves" with "Most moves iterated on 5-6 times", Baby Steps' walking system evolved over five years, and Siege's destruction team was "dedicated to destruction for ~5 years" ([MCV/Develop on Dying Light](https://mcvuk.com/development-news/the-vaulting-dead-implementing-first-person-parkour-in-dying-light/); [Game Developer, Mirror's Edge interview](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/the-philosophy-of-faith-a-mirror-s-edge-interview); [Game Rant, Baby Steps interview](https://gamerant.com/baby-steps-interview-gamble-walking-gameplay-only/); [L'Heureux, GDC 2016 PDF](https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2016/Presentations/LHeureux_Julien_Art_Of_Destruction.pdf)). Durations are therefore replaced by exit criteria; a team that must schedule should state its size and use those analogues. Every phase is ordered so that later systems consume numbers earlier ones measured, and the first deliverable runs locally with no content pipeline.

**Phase 0, engine verification, is delivered.** The artifact is `design/prototype/phase0_joint_drive/` with its `README.md`, `project.godot` (Jolt, 120 Hz, `max_physics_steps_per_frame 16`, interpolation on), `main.gd`, `run_tests.sh` and three tests; it reproduces with the build command in the runtime note (5–10 minutes on four cores; `disable_path_overrides=no` for a template build), `run_tests.sh bin/<binary>` in about 3 s with exit code 0, and `--test=chain_bench` in real time in 25 s, with an `override.cfg` of `[threading] worker_pool/max_threads=1` for the single-thread row (`godot_runtime_verification.md` §8; `design/prototype/phase0_joint_drive/README.md`). It established the API names, the drive's tracking error and tick-rate independence, the torque cap's exactness, the feed-forward sign, the collision-exception requirement, the `skeleton_updated` read rule, the sleep behaviour, and the per-step cost; its API-presence table tells any build within a second whether PR #118997 is present. Two of the first edition's Phase 0 items are closed without work: the fork has no local physics changes, and zero-damping springs are legal while zero-stiffness ones are inert.

**Phase 0b, remaining engine checks (exit: each item measured or bound).** Cherry-pick PR #123875 and confirm `get_applied_force` on a loaded knee joint reads the expected static load. Call `PhysicsServer3D.body_set_max_contacts_reported` on a bone RID and record whether `_integrate_forces` on a `PhysicalBone3D` receives contacts; if not, the damage model's hit-location input is a shape query at the Δv spike. Run the identical-input replay test: the same input stream twice through the phase0 chain and a standing rig, diffing the pelvis trajectory per tick; zero divergence is expected under Jolt, any divergence is located in the Godot layer. Add the 6DOF cone-swing option or the cone-twist `Position` motor, and the `MassNormalizedStiffnessAndDamping` binding, each with its XML. Fill `get_process_info` so the physics monitors read. Build a rendered (non-headless) binary and observe whether the skinned mesh steps at tick rate at 60 versus 120 Hz with interpolation on. Measure the frame budget on the "TPS bridge" scene with a 15-body ragdoll at 60 / 120 / 240 Hz, 50–500 awake shards and a 2,048-instance `MultiMesh`, reporting physics, BLAS refit/build and TLAS separately. Land nothing in `modules/jolt_physics` that upstream is already merging.

**Milestone 1, the locally testable prototype (exit: the acceptance tests below pass in a grey-box room).** The room has a floor, a 1 m step, a 2 m and a 4 m ledge with a 1 m graspable face under the 4 m one, a wall and a slope. The rig is the 70.4 kg CEF humanoid on `PhysicalBone3D` 6DOF drives at the CEF gains with per-axis scales, joint collisions disabled per bone, driven each tick from a hidden intent skeleton; the intent capsule with Quake acceleration and Godot's `CharacterBody3D` defaults; the pelvis authority spring with k_V = 100 N·s/m and a force limit; the SIMBICON four-state walk with cd 0.5 / cv 0.2, Coros's pendulum step capped at 0.6 L, Stephens' ankle/hip/step decision, gravity compensation, Euphoria's abort rule (lean 0.6, COM within 0.5 m of the lowest foot) ramping the `Position` motors down into the Overgrowth three-layer fall pose with per-state strength, and the 1 s recovery once `|v|² < 20`; the three-layer camera with Source's punch constants; the RMB grab as a force-limited slipping 6DOF constraint with a 0.96 kN budget; the damage read-out from per-tick Δv and, once PR #123875 is in, joint lambdas. Acceptance: absorb the SIMBICON 3D push set (0.4 s at chest height: 340 N forward, 270 N backward, 330 / 240 N lateral, the four diagonals) and Coros's 600 N × 0.1 s omnidirectional push at 0.6 m/s; find the DeepMimic push limit in 10 N steps (walk 240 N forward / 300 N sideways is the published reference); survive a 5 m/s cube per second (SuperTrack); catch the 2 m ledge at the apex of a standing jump; **fail** to hard-catch the 4 m ledge arriving at 6.3 m/s, then catch it as a 1 m slide when arriving at ≤ 3 m/s onto the graspable face; keep the ledge scan at ≤ 8 queries per tick; keep the camera's summed punch ≤ 12° (this report's inference) and the anchor offset ≤ 15 cm; measure the input-latency chain and set a target; ship a gamepad map; and answer the one question no source answers, whether a spring-chased ragdoll feels like a body or a puppet on an elastic, with a playtest note. Cartwheel-3d (Apache-2.0) is the licence-friendly reference implementation; SIMBICON-CEF (GPLv3) is for its controller files only ([Banbury/cartwheel-3d](https://github.com/Banbury/cartwheel-3d); [simbicon_cef](https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~van/simbicon_cef/)).

**Milestone 2, the competence scalar and the opening (exit: the rebuilt table is live and measured).** Expose every knob in the competence table as a curve of s (torque cap with retiming, added delay, cd/cv, step width, arm pose, ankle push-off switch, pendulum switch, fall-rate target), keep the drunk and fatigue channels separate, and measure falls per hundred steps against the infant baseline (1 per 46–69 novice, 1 per 136 experienced) and minutes-to-competent-walking against the MGSV threshold; verify the seeded wobble reproduces under input replay; decide and implement the assist path; audit against Foddy's flavours and Fan's rules; keep the required path short and move clumsy mastery into optional content.

**Milestone 3, ladders V and H to tier 3 with the damage model and the death loop (exit: the tables reproduce).** Jump and sprint multipliers; Ha's landing controller with the roll, validated at ≥ 40 % peak-force reduction against a stiff landing (Puddle & Maulder 43 %, Standing & Maulder 40–49 %); brace as a compliance-distance change; self-arrest as a braking-force budget per stance and a raised k_V; durability multipliers on the per-leg thresholds; the Eiband-duration and HIC-based injury classification with Rosén and Hussain as km/h sanity columns; landing and wall tables reproduced within tick resolution from lambdas and Δv; checkpoints at every gate, rewind ≤ 30 s, persistent injury as a durability debuff, state-transition cancels closed; impact audio and rumble tiers; the fall-damage toggle; consequence tutorials per tier.

**Milestone 4, destruction tiers 1–3 (exit: a wall breaks by the numbers and survives a save).** Reactive dressing, pre-fractured swap, then the bond graph with impulse decomposition, baseline-subtracted break test, island flood-fill, a `MultiMesh` debris pool and hardness anchors (drywall 489 N / 67 J, doors 80–200 J per blow, brick 500 J / 270 N·s for rigid impactors, with the striking part's effective mass as the game parameter); the event log as the save and replay format; the frame budget within the envelope measured in Phase 0b on the TPS bridge; optionally the Energized-RBF impulse on break.

**Milestone 5, the Houdini pipeline, parallel with 3–4 (exit: an asset round-trips).** Houdini 22 RBD Material Fracture → constraint graph → glTF extras (or a hython sidecar on Houdini 21) → `EditorScenePostImport`/`GLTFDocumentExtension` building the destructible and its graph; Ragdoll Collision Shapes and Configure Joints → Post-Write Script → bone metadata → a `PhysicalBone3D` builder; HeightField Output → Terrain3D built from source for 4.8; scatter points → `MultiMesh`; a PDG wedge batch of strength variants.

**Milestone 6, upper tiers and scale (exit: a flight regime change is a frame change).** Wall-run, ground pound, Immovable and the world-response state; a `precision=double` build; a co-moving physics space with bulk-velocity subtraction at each regime boundary, raised caps for unconstrained debris only, an origin rebase in project code with the GPU-particle and trail gap noted; snapshot-billboard collapses for large structures; the comfort layer (dynamic FOV, haze, snap turns). Only after Milestone 3 is stable should a learned residual be considered, and then for NPCs first.

## The supplied sources, reassessed

The user supplied twelve links plus two named papers; the second pass re-opened what it could and changed four verdicts in detail, none in kind.

| Supplied item | Identified as | Second-edition verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Source 1 (biped control) | Reil & Massey, "Biologically inspired control of physically simulated bipeds", *Theory in Biosciences* 120(3–4), 2001 (Springer, unopened; Crossref DOI 10.1007/s12064-001-0026-8) | Lineage only: the founders' CPG + reflex + genetic algorithm one year before the open companion. Its companion Reil & Husbands 2002 **is** open (10 pages): 13 bodies, 11 joints, PD actuators "essentially equivalent to damped torsional springs", CTRNN of ten neurons at 0.02 s; its PD constants sit in an equation image and were not extracted ([Reil & Husbands 2002 PDF](https://users.sussex.ac.uk/~philh/pubs/reilhusbandsIEEE.pdf); [Crossref record](https://api.crossref.org/works/10.1007/s12064-001-0026-8)). |
| Source 2 (biped control) | Van Ham's VUB PhD on the MACCEPA actuator; canonical version *Robotics and Autonomous Systems* 55(10), 2007 | Adjacent: independently controllable equilibrium angle and stiffness, a clean mental model for "PD target and PD gain as two separately animated channels"; the thesis remains unreachable by every route ([RAS 2007 DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.robot.2007.03.001)). |
| Source 3 (biped control) | Saputra et al., "Biologically Inspired Control System for 3-D Locomotion of a Humanoid Biped Robot", *IEEE TSMC: Systems* 46(7), 2016 | Adjacent: "gain neurons" scaling joint energy and "command neurons" setting step length are a tidy abstraction for a power level and an intended step; abstract only ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/TSMC.2015.2497250)). |
| Source 4 (biped control) | Yilei Gao, Manchester PhD 2024 on biologically inspired bipedal control from human gait data | Adjacent: a staged torque → reflex → muscle development order; PDF Cloudflare-gated ([Manchester record](https://research.manchester.ac.uk/en/studentTheses/design-of-biologically-inspired-control-algorithm-for-bipedal-mod/)). |
| Source 5 (learned control) | Tollola, "Procedural animations in interactive art experiences", arXiv 2105.09153 (draft) | **Off-topic**: projection mapping and museum installations; no physics, ragdolls or learning ([arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.09153)). |
| Source 6 (learned control) | Guerraz et al., "A Procedural Approach to Animate Interactive Natural Sceneries", CASA 2003 | Off-topic for control, useful for tier-one reactivity: a "tread on grass" field and crush-recovery timer as cheap footprint feedback ([EVASION PDF](http://evasion.imag.fr/Publications/2003/GPRFC03/paper.pdf)). |
| Source 7 (procedural animation) | Pardeshi et al., *IJMCR* 14(SI 03):110–114, 14 Mar 2026, CC BY 4.0 (Zenodo record confirmed) | Weak: a five-page position paper whose only equation is `y(t) = A sin(ωt + φ)`; its reference list is the useful part ([Zenodo record](https://zenodo.org/records/19510905)). |
| Source 8 (procedural animation) | Henry Mead, independent student report on Academia.edu | Not a citable technical primary ([Academia page](https://www.academia.edu/175308276/Procedural_Character_Animation_for_Games)). |
| Source 9 (procedural animation) | Deglorie et al., GRAPP 2015 | **Useful**, units now exact: CPU ticks per frame on an i7-2600 (Jacobian transpose 1,250, CCD 300, fuzzy 800) with errors in centimetres (0.53 / 0.72 / 1.70) ([SciTePress PDF](https://www.scitepress.org/papers/2015/53106/53106.pdf)). |
| Source 10 (procedural animation) | Academia.edu topic listing | Could not be opened (403 / CAPTCHA) ([listing](https://www.academia.edu/Documents/in/Procedural_Animation)). |
| Source 11 (procedural animation) | Jarl Larsson, Blekinge MSc 2015 (urn:nbn:se:bth-1832) | Now identified from its abstract and code: a pose-driven-feedback Bullet torque controller measured for 1–100 characters with "a relatively small performance impact that scales almost linearly"; code is MIT (`jarllarsson/promenade`); the PDF itself is still unreachable, and its contribution is a scaling study, not a gait-quality source ([CORE record](https://api.core.ac.uk/v3/search/works?q=title:%22Performance%20of%20Physics-Driven%20Procedural%20Animation%22&limit=3); [jarllarsson/promenade](https://github.com/jarllarsson/promenade)). |
| Source 12 (procedural animation) | Yuke Fu, Lund MSc 2025 (full text fileOId 9206770) | **Useful** with a caveat: the CPG equations are the background chapter's textbook forms; the implemented framework, foot probe, 13 parameters and 29-person study are the thesis's own ([LUP full text](https://lup.lub.lu.se/luur/download?func=downloadFile&recordOId=9206764&fileOId=9206770)). |
| Named: Motion VAEs (Ling et al. 2020) | Kinematic generative model | Kinematic only; "does not simulate physics" ([MVAE PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2103.14274)). |
| Named: Learned Motion Matching (Holden et al. 2020) | Replaces the motion-matching database with three networks | Kinematic; now with its Table 3: locomotion MM 52.1 MB at ≈ 9 µs per frame versus LMM 5.3 MB at ≈ 197 µs, so LMM pays only when memory is the constraint ([LMM PDF](https://theorangeduck.com/media/uploads/other_stuff/Learned_Motion_Matching.pdf)). |

The four biped-control sources are "biologically inspired" in the robotics sense and none publishes copyable gains; the SIMBICON/Coros/Hodgins family remains the engineering starting point, with these cited for CPG, reflex and compliant-actuator framing. Of the six procedural-animation sources only Deglorie 2015 and Fu 2025 are substantive primaries.

## What changed since the first edition

Every correction below comes from the red-team review (`report_red_team_review.md`, ranked verdict §5.1 and consolidated list §5.3), the "Contradictions" sections and corrected verification rows of the thirteen `*_pass2.md` notes, or the runtime note; the source column names the primary each corrected value now rests on. Items are grouped by the red team's severity.

### Blocking

| First edition | Second edition | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Damage model reads `PhysicsDirectBodyState3D.get_contact_impulse` on a `RigidBody3D` with `contact_monitor` | `PhysicalBone3D` has no contact monitor; Jolt impulses are estimates on any multi-contact body; head and chest scored from per-tick Δv (duration-aware), limbs and grip from joint lambdas via PR #123875, contacts for location only | [Godot docs, Using Jolt Physics](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/latest/tutorials/physics/using_jolt_physics.html); `doc/classes/PhysicalBone3D.xml`; [godot PR #123875](https://github.com/godotengine/godot/pull/123875); [Ha, Ye, Liu 2012](https://www.dropbox.com/s/6qh29mlcty5xqqb/2012_landing.pdf) |
| Fall damage "uses the 6 m safe / 18 m fatal intent" copied from Source; BLUF said tibiae break "above roughly 6 m" while the body said 3 m; V-ladder mixed Niu (two-leg) and the rigid model | Niu is per foot, valid to 1.03 m; one model per row, per leg, with the 1.5–2.5× peak band; fracture risk begins at ≈ 3 m, likely by 5–6 m, tibia exceeded by 10 m; the Source height intent is not copied | [Niu 2014](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4160626/); [Funk 2002](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12596644/); [Seegmiller & McCaw 2003](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC314389/); [Gallenberger 2013](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3861818/) |
| Ledge catch ceiling "3–4 m/s" from "1–2 BW eccentric arm capacity" and harness forces; grab implemented with a 1 kN limit that tears free | Two grips = 0.96 kN catch ≈ 2 m/s (0.2 m drop); harness figures describe a rope; the grab is a force-limited slipping constraint (0.45–1.8 m slide at human strength) plus a level rule; a 3 m drop is rescued by the landing controller, not the grab | [Bohannon & Magasi 2015](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24729356/); [Petzl, forces in a real fall](https://www.petzl.com/US/en/Sport/Forces-at-work-in-a-real-fall) |
| Top tier adopted Megaton Rainfall's casualty bar, scoring on time plus casualties and mission-variety advice | Mission-loop material removed; world consequence stands on Superman Returns' damage meter, Hulk: Ultimate Destruction and Battlefield 6's design statements; Megaton cited for capabilities and engineering only | [Superman Returns, Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superman_Returns_(video_game)); [EA on Battlefield 6](https://www.ea.com/news/how-battlefield-6-redefined-destruction) |
| Competence table: torque 40–50 % at s = 0 → 100 % at s = 1, citing Learning to Get Up | The paper finds 40–60 % natural and > 70 % erratic; toddler is weak and slow (≈ 40 % with retiming κ), competent ≈ 60 %, strength above it is the S ladder; SIMBICON's 90 N·m collapse floor is the SI anchor | [Learning to Get Up](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.00307); [SIMBICON](https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~van/papers/2007-siggraph-simbicon.pdf) |

### Major

| First edition | Second edition | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 120 Hz derived from "Jolt's stiff 20 Hz drive needs ≥ 80 Hz by Catto's four-steps rule" and "classical PD only worked at 0.1–0.5 ms" | Catto's rule is for explicit force springs; Jolt's bound is (0, 0.5 × sim freq]; SIMBICON 3D ran at 5 ms and Hämäläinen at 1/30 s with velocity motors; measured drive error identical at 60/120/240 Hz; 120 Hz kept for contact fidelity, headroom and the un-interpolated skinned mesh | [Catto GDC 2011](https://box2d.org/files/ErinCatto_SoftConstraints_GDC2011.pdf); [Jolt docs](https://jrouwe.github.io/JoltPhysics/index.html); [Hämäläinen 2014](https://mediatech.aalto.fi/publications/graphics/OnlineSMC/preprint.pdf); `godot_runtime_verification.md` |
| Stable PD proposed for virtual forces, gravity compensation and the balance feed-forward | SPD is a joint-PD reformulation in generalised coordinates and does not apply to Jacobian-transpose terms; under solver-side drives the balance law needs no script torque | [Tan, Liu, Turk 2011](https://www.cc.gatech.edu/~turk/my_papers/stable_pd.pdf) |
| "Check whether the spring counts as active with zero damping" | Stiffness > 0 is required, damping may be 0; `PhysicalBone3D` default stiffness 0.0 drives nothing | `SixDOFConstraint.cpp:571-577`; `SpringSettings.h:41`; `physical_bone_3d.h` |
| H-ladder cells said "lethal" and "Rosén ≈ 5 %" together; H3 braced "Bräck-level"; H1 "past every regulatory limit even braced"; death drawn from Rosén–Sander at any compliance; "Stapp's voluntary 46 g over 1.4 s" | Readings from the model against Eiband's duration curve (45 g @ 0.044 s … 13 g @ 0.6 s), FMVSS chest 60 g and HIC; Rosén and Hussain as km/h sanity columns; braced H1 is 26 g, braced H3 is 68 g | [Eiband 1959](https://ntrs.nasa.gov/api/citations/19980228043/downloads/19980228043.pdf); [49 CFR 571.208](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2023-title49-vol6/xml/CFR-2023-title49-vol6-sec571-208.xml); [Hussain 2019](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31176144/) |
| Brick wall breaks at 270 N·s, making an unpowered sprinter (480 N·s at 80 kg) "marginal", while the text said humans cannot break masonry | The boulder threshold applies to rigid impactors; a body's contact impulse is the striking part's (few kg fist, 5–10 kg shoulder); masonry stays at S3; one mass, 70 kg, throughout | [Mavrouli 2024](https://doi.org/10.3390/geosciences14090226); [Walilko 2005](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16183766/) |
| Competence rows: "step-time CoV 0.07 → 0.03"; "1.10 → 1.43 m/s"; arms "≈ 80° → reciprocal swing ≈ 30°"; hip 42.5° → 29.1°; delay "200–300 ms → 0"; fall rate "1 per 69 steps" | Stride-*velocity* CoV; 1.43 m/s was healthy elderly; 47–49° elevation after 15 weeks and reciprocal swing by 18 months; bilateral hip ≈ 41° → 31°; *added* delay on an inherent 100–160 ms; 69.2 is the per-infant mean, 46 the ratio of means, 136 pooled | [Kroneberg 2024](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11174553/); [Gimunová 2021](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8744759/); [Rasman 2021](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8480973/); [Adolph 2012](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3591461/) |
| Two-rig system "in the pattern Boneworks and Ubisoft's motion-matching work both converged on"; pelvis spring "Euphoria's cheat force" `ResistAcc` | Boneworks' rig is a VR body (6 → 11 → 18 rigidbodies) and Clavet's is kinematic; the precedents are TABS, the EA patent, Coros's k_V and Neural Assistive Impulses; `ResistAcc` resists floor acceleration | [Laatsch on X (mirror)](https://x.com/BrandonJLa/status/2009741997897044046); [Game Developer on TABS](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/how-landfall-games-finds-the-fun-in-physics-engines); [US7403202B1](https://patents.google.com/patent/US7403202B1/en) |
| One `Skeleton3D` carrying IK and the simulator | Two skeletons (intent and simulated); the simulated pose is script-readable only inside `skeleton_updated` | `godot_runtime_verification.md` §4.2; `doc/classes/SkeletonModifier3D.xml` |
| Milestone durations "(days)", "(weeks)", "(months)"; "a few hundred lines" | Exit criteria; analogues (Dying Light 1.5 years, Mirror's Edge 2-week cycles, Baby Steps 5 years, Siege 5 years) | [MCV/Develop on Dying Light](https://mcvuk.com/development-news/the-vaulting-dead-implementing-first-person-parkour-in-dying-light/); [L'Heureux GDC 2016](https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2016/Presentations/LHeureux_Julien_Art_Of_Destruction.pdf) |
| "The wobble is fully reproducible" and "determinism is not guaranteed by Godot's Jolt integration" in the same report | Jolt is deterministic; the caveat was the extension's README; Godot's layer is untested; replay defined as input replay plus snapshots with an event log for destruction | [Jolt README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jrouwe/JoltPhysics/master/README.md); [godot-jolt README](https://github.com/godot-jolt/godot-jolt) |
| Overgrowth's ERP/CFM ramp maps to "a 6DOF constraint with zero angular limits and soft-limit spring settings" | Jolt has translation-only limit springs; the ramp is the `Position` motor's frequency, damping or torque limit | `SixDOFConstraint.h:81-83`; `jolt_generic_6dof_joint_3d.cpp:425-445, 724-726` |
| H2 self-arrest by capture step `d = v·sqrt(h/g + v²/4g²)` | 8.7 m single step at 12.34 m/s; replaced by a per-stance braking-force budget scaled from Harper; capture point stays in the balance controller | [Pratt 2006](https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~cga/legs/Pratt_Goswami_Humanoids2006.pdf); [Harper 2022](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9474351/) |
| "runs at 120 Hz on one core" | Measured: ≈ 80 µs per step single-threaded, ≈ 200 µs on four threads, for 15 bodies; no controller was measured at 120 Hz in any note | `godot_runtime_verification.md` §4.3 |
