| Milestone 1 acceptance "0.1 s 600 N forward / 500 N backward push (SIMBICON)" | Those are the 2D biped's; the 3D set is 0.4 s at 340 / 270 / 330 / 240 N with diagonals; Coros's 600 N × 0.1 s is 3D | [SIMBICON](https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~van/papers/2007-siggraph-simbicon.pdf) §7.2; [Coros 2010](https://www.cs.ubc.ca/~van/papers/2010-TOG-gbwc/paper.pdf) |
| "Impacts follow Zordan's recipe: drop the tracking gains for a 0.1–0.2 s reaction delay" | Zordan 2005 holds gains fixed and delays the target switch; it is an offline pipeline; the gain drop is Zordan & Hodgins 2002 via the survey and SIMBICON's 150 ms FEL delay | [Zordan 2005](http://strangepaths.com/forum/files/Zordan2005.pdf); [Geijtenbeek & Pronost 2012](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-8659.2012.03189.x) |
| EA patent "halves per-part force and torque limits during collisions and minimises them on ground contact"; balance by "support polygon of the feet" | Strength scheduled per supervisor state (half when falling, small when staying down, nearly all on standby when getting up); support circle from shin/foot COMs with an animation-agreement check | [US7403202B1](https://patents.google.com/patent/US7403202B1/en) |
| Crackdown 3 "Resilience" as impact-tolerance precedent (H5, S4) and the "Ground Pound precedes Resilience" ordering example | Resilience is elemental resistance; ordering example is Prototype's survivability pricing; Saints Row IV sells damage reduction first | [Shacknews, Crackdown 3](https://www.shacknews.com/article/109880/skills-abilities-and-leveling-fast-in-crackdown-3); [Altered Gamer, Prototype](https://www.alteredgamer.com/prototype/39802-powers-movement-and-survivability-guide/) |
| Saints Row IV "knocks them over but you can't be knocked over" as H5; parameter names "printed in the upgrade text" as cluster purchases | That row is marked Disabled; the obtainable relative is "Super Jump – Immovable Object"; parameter-line upgrades are Rift medal rewards transcribed from `unlockables.xtbl` | [Saints Row Wiki, Super Powers](https://saintsrow.fandom.com/wiki/Super_Powers_in_Saints_Row_IV); [saintsrowmods.com](https://www.saintsrowmods.com/forum/threads/changing-glide-terminal-velocity.3816/) |
| "only DReCon and SuperTrack were ever trained in a game-class engine"; CALM's "failure on stairs"; "native ONNX GDExtension unmaintained since Feb 2024"; "two to three orders of magnitude below Isaac Gym" | DeepMimic and AMP ran in Bullet at 1.2 kHz; CALM anticipates; a maintained ORT loader and an ncnn runner exist; the gap for a DReCon-sized residual is about one order of magnitude | [DeepMimic](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1804.02717); [CALM](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.02195); [godot-onnx-loader](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DynamicDevices/godot-onnx-loader/main/README.md); [Isaac Gym](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.10470) |

### Coverage added

Death and respawn loop; per-frame budget against the ray-tracing renderer; determinism and replay; impact audio and haptics; save and persistence of destruction; the ledge-detection algorithm; the input-latency chain; the accessibility and assist path; a gamepad map; a stated exclusion of multiplayer (the design is single-player; the event-log and determinism choices do not preclude it); NPC crowd reactions, swimming, fall damage on other bodies, a diegetic reason for the powers and the ground-to-flight transition of the ragdoll remain open (below).

## Open questions, research gaps, and where to look next

The second pass ran out of search budget in most subtopics and several primaries stayed blocked from this environment; the gaps below are ranked by how much they could still change the plan, with the tools that worked.

**Engine facts still to measure locally (Phase 0b).** Whether `_integrate_forces` on a `PhysicalBone3D` receives contacts after `body_set_max_contacts_reported` on the bone RID; the identical-input replay diff; the cost and stability of a full humanoid with realistic limits, self-contact and ground contact rather than a hanging chain, and the thread-count effect in a populated scene; the hinge motor's step-dependent conversion and stiff drives under contact; targets under enabled angular limits (Jolt clamps to the swing/twist limits, `SixDOFConstraint.cpp:343-348`); the `_reload_joint()` parameter-loss inference; the skinned mesh's stepping under interpolation in a rendered build; and the frame budget on the TPS bridge. The Jolt job-system warning under batched ticks (`jolt_job_system.cpp:124`) deserves a look during the Phase 0b patch work. Working tools: GitHub `pull/<n>` and `issues/<n>` HTML pages, `commits/master/<path>` listings and the `.atom` feeds; the GitHub MCP `search_pull_requests` unscoped; Godot forum Discourse JSON (`/search.json?q=…`, `/t/<id>.json`); `api.github.com/search/repositories` (the `repos/` endpoint returns 403 here). Queries to run next: `repo:godotengine/godot is:open "ESwingType" OR "swing type"`; `repo:godotengine/godot-proposals SwingTwist OR "cone twist motor" OR "position motor"`; `repo:jrouwe/JoltPhysics is:issue Godot ragdoll motor frequency`; forum `?q=set_angular_target_rotation`, `?q=PositionAndVelocity`; GitHub code search for `generic_6dof_joint_set_angular_target_rotation` and `has_target_rotation` once it stops returning 502; Jolt Discussions #1764 and godot-jolt Discussions #843, #856, #561; kickback and emre-f issues for Jolt tuning reports.

**Euphoria's internals and the Force Unleashed coupling.** No primary states Euphoria's simulation rate or per-character budget. Venues: Digital Foundry's Backbreaker and GTA IV PS3 coverage, Develop magazine 2007–2010 back issues, Edge's "Making of GTA IV", the 2009 developer-interview video with an auto-transcript, the Scribd white paper via a rendering viewer, Bullet forum threads t=2194 and t=1870 when pybullet.org is up; the remaining ScriptHookVDotNet helper pages (`StaggerFallHelper`, `SmartFallHelper`, `RollDownStairsHelper`, `ShotHelper`, `StayUprightHelper`, `PedalLegsHelper`); Google Patents `assignee:naturalmotion` and the Zynga-assigned US 9,449,416 B2; for TFU, GDC Vault free content 2008–2009, Game Developer magazine 2008 postmortems, and CGW 2010 for TFU II. Havok's `hkaKeyFrameHierarchyUtility.h` (fields `m_hierarchyGain`, `m_velocityDamping`, `m_accelerationGain`, `m_velocityGain`, `m_positionGain`, `m_snapGain`) is the published numeric heart of a velocity-based ragdoll follower and was not found in the vendored SDK tried. The Uncharted 4 talk's Vault ID is 1024087 or 1024594 (both listed until one is confirmed dead); Boneworks' HMD-to-head coupling has no SLZ primary (try the Oculus Connect 6 talk and the MarrowSDK repository).

**Biomechanics that affect thresholds.** No study measures hand or arm forces in a parkour arm jump or a "hanging drop", so the catch table remains derived; no in-vivo glenohumeral dislocation force with muscle tone exists; the intact fifth metacarpal's fracture load is in a 2023 JAAOS figure but not in extractable text; the A2 pulley's ≈ 400 N stays secondary; one-arm hang has no duration norm; NHTSA's "Development of Improved Injury Criteria" returned 403 at five mirrors, so the HIC-to-AIS curve rests on Prasad & Mertz 1985 and Mertz, Irwin, Prasad 2003/2016 (abstracts only); Rabita 2015's F0 and v0 values for a linear force–velocity sprint model are in the full text only; Sutherland's age tables are image-only (Sutherland 1997 in *Gait & Posture* or the 1988 book reproduce them); no adult intoxication study reports stride-time variability against BAC; body compliance distances against a barrier are unmeasured. Terms and venues: *Stapp Car Crash Journal* ("Kennedy 2004 femur bending", "Rupp 2002 hip knee loading", "Kuppa lower extremity injury criteria"), *Ann Adv Automot Med*, IRCOBI proceedings (free PDFs), "Eiband 1959 NASA Memo 5-19-59E", "Stapp 1955 J Aviation Medicine 26 268", "Snyder 1963 CARI 63-15", "Hussain 2019 meta-analysis pedestrian fatality", "Vigouroux 2006 J Biomech pulley forces crimp slope values", "Toussaint Schepens 2024 landing stiffness damping", "Standing Maulder 2015 traceurs", "NSCA percentile vertical jump", "ASTM F476-14 door impact 200 J grade", "Biewener 1990 Science 250 1097 safety factor". Europe PMC's `fullTextXML` endpoint and PubMed E-utilities worked for every open paper; Crossref resolves DOIs without a key; OpenAlex needs an API key on this network and Semantic Scholar's search endpoint throttles, while its `/paper/{id}/citations` endpoint works at about one call per 5 s.

**Camera and comfort.** No flat-screen study quantifies head-bob amplitude or roll tolerance; every quantified study is HMD-based. Rockstar's first-person rationale (Newswire is JavaScript-only, Wayback blocked), Stress Level Zero's head-coupling statement, Titanfall 2's tilt numbers, Terenzi & Zaal's Table 4 units, Half-Life 1's engine default for `rollangle`, and the 0.75 in/unit convention are all unresolved. Search *Displays*, *Applied Ergonomics*, *Human Factors* and *Ergonomics* for "view bob amplitude", "camera oscillation amplitude nausea desktop", "first-person shooter monitor visually induced motion sickness field of view"; chain from Allison & Palmisano 2025's reference list; use Combine OverWiki for Alyx commentary, PC Gamer 2020-04-07 for Valve's deep-dive quotes, and the `api.fxtwitter.com/<user>/status/<id>` mirror for tweets.

**Movement, gating and precedents.** Dying Light's ledge criteria are in Kulon's GDC 2018 video (Vault 1025208); Minecraft's elytra formula units need a `flyIntoWall` code search; Breath of the Wild's per-second stamina drain needs zeldamods or a speedrun wiki; Cyberpunk's landing tiers are TweakDB records on blocked Nexus pages; Titanfall 2's constants are player-measured kph; Crackdown's fall-damage behaviour is in no opened source; Saints Row IV's `unlockables.xtbl` would settle the parameter names; Boss Keys' dependency graphs are behind a consent wall; Prototype has no design statement on its power curve; Control's Ground Slam prerequisite is unresolved between a snippet and a wiki. GDC Vault ids that opened: 1387 (Mirror's Edge), 1023559 (Pittman), 1021116 (Catto), 1022114 (England), 1025208 (Kulon), 1015704 (Foddy), 1024307 (Celeste), 1026422 / 1026084 (Spider-Man), 1021789 (Sunset Overdrive locomotion), 1023948, 1023329, 1024587, 1015568, 1012242 (reward and motivation talks); slide PDFs follow `media.gdcvault.com/gdc<year>/Presentations/<Surname>_<Name>_<Title>.pdf` (2016) and `…/gdc<year>/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/…` (2024).

**Destruction and pipeline.** Unreal's "Chaos Destruction Optimization" page is JavaScript-only; Volition's GDC 2009 slides do not exist online; Havok Destruction's API is not public; Control's chunk and joint thresholds are video-only; Gustafsson's structural-integrity post is unreadable; DMM's licensing terms and support are answerable only by e-mail; Breaking Good's runtime timings are behind ACM's 403; whether Godot's Jolt module exposes `MutableCompoundShape` and whether `Mesh.convex_decompose` should be bound are fork questions; The Finals' per-frame budgets are in the video; a Godot + Jolt bodies-per-millisecond benchmark does not exist. For Houdini: whether the 22.0 glTF ROP writes `gltf_node_extras` for nodes built from packed primitives via the path attribute, the Character ROP's Post-Write Script route, glue `strength` units, HEGo against a 4.8-dev godot-cpp, Terrain3D and HTerrain on 4.8-dev, and any SideFX statement on Indie use of third-party HAPI hosts (watch the "Houdini for Realtime" subforum). Papers to chain next via Semantic Scholar: VBD (10.1145/3658179), AVBD (10.1145/3731195), Energized RBF (10.1145/3203207), the bonded-DEM pair (10.1109/TVCG.2021.3106738, 10.1145/3711852), STA-FEM (arXiv 2605.11673). Direct copies: O'Brien's papers at `graphics.berkeley.edu/papers/<Key>/<Key>.pdf`, Müller at `matthias-research.github.io`, Utah's VBD/AVBD project pages.

**Megaton Rainfall and scale.** The six PSVR interfaces, FOV, motion blur, camera smoothing, the precision scheme, LOD distances, city cell size and destruction persistence are undocumented; the IGF 2015 entry text and the IndieDB "Mach 10" text need a browser on archive.org; Thorne 2005's abstract is behind IEEE's challenge page; Frontier's supercruise design rationale has no primary; the Asobo and Hello Games talks are description-only. Try `research-repository.uwa.edu.au` for Thorne's thesis, Frontier's "Design Decision Forum" archives, the KSP API pages for `FloatingOrigin.Threshold` and `Krakensbane`, Egosoft's dev diaries on multiple physics worlds, and Godot PRs #66178, #99986, #100896, #100319 with tracker #98655 and proposals #892, #15350, #15436.

**Learned control.** Won et al. 2022's full text is paywalled everywhere reachable; ARC Raiders' production simulator, network sizes and control rate are unpublished (the GDC Vault recording and the AI and Games keynote of 10–11 November 2026 are the next venues); no benchmark measures ORT, ncnn or godot-infer latency inside a Godot physics tick; no paper trains, evaluates or transfers a controller in Jolt (an arXiv-only negative); UniCon's time step and Stubborn's hardware are not stated; SIGGRAPH 2026's motion-control session still has placeholders on the Ke-Sen list. Seeds worth chaining: ADD (2505.04961), AMOR (2505.23708), InstantMimic (2609.09821), Neural Assistive Impulses (2604.05394), PDP (2406.00960); arXiv's HTML search works when its API returns 406.

**Design questions no source answers.** Whether a spring-chased ragdoll reads as a body or a puppet; whether players tolerate real inertia in first person once telegraphs are in (DICE bet no; the brief bets yes); how the 15-body ragdoll behaves in flight and whether the authority spring is the flight model; a diegetic reason for the powers; NPC and crowd reactions and an NPC ragdoll budget; swimming as the "soft surface"; fall damage on other bodies; VR, which changes the camera stack fundamentally and should be decided once.

## Conclusion

Two things are different at the end of the second pass. The first is that the central engineering bet has been cashed: the fork's solver-side orientation drive is not a source-read hope but a measured behaviour, tick-rate independent, exactly capped, cheap, and reachable through the node the ragdoll must be built from, with two sharp edges (joint collisions and the `skeleton_updated` read rule) that would have cost days to find later. What the runtime test removed is the argument for high tick rates from spring stability; what it left is a plainer reason for 120 Hz, contact fidelity for fast bodies and a skinned mesh that steps at the physics rate, and a number, about 200 µs per step for the whole character, that makes the choice cheap either way. The second is that the design's physics is now honest at its weakest joints. The first edition's promise that "every power is gated by its own consequence" was true in principle and false in four numbers: a copied safe height that forgave fractures, a grab that caught what no hand can hold, a death draw that could not tell a braced stop from a rigid one, and a competence curve that made mastery look erratic. Each is now derived from the quantities the rig actually exposes, per-tick velocity change and joint constraint force, against duration-aware tolerances and per-leg thresholds, and the consequences got harsher in exactly the way the brief wanted: a frail body can hurt itself from two metres, a human catch is a slide down a wall rather than a snatch at a ledge, and masonry stays out of reach until the body is stiff enough to count as a boulder.

The remaining risk has moved from the engine to the feel and the frame. Nothing in the sources says whether a body chased by a spring reads as weight or as elastic, whether first-person players accept the inertia this design sells as fun, or what a ragdoll that refits its acceleration structure every frame costs a ray-tracing renderer that already spent 4.3 ms on idle actors before the fork's own gating fix. Those are Milestone 1 and Phase 0b measurements, not literature questions, which is why the plan now leads with a delivered test project and a list of exit criteria rather than estimated weeks. The larger implication is methodological: a design that claims physical honesty has to be audited the way this one was, by recomputing its tables, reading its sources' units, and running its engine, and the second edition's audit trail exists so that the next pass can do the same to it.

Local engine files and the project's own notes and artifacts follow the web sources.

### Project notes and artifacts this edition rests on

- `design/prototype/phase0_joint_drive/` (`README.md`, `project.godot`, `main.gd`, `main.tscn`, `run_tests.sh`, `tests/drive_test.gd`, `tests/pendulum_test.gd`, `tests/physical_bone_test.gd`, `tests/chain_benchmark.gd`): the delivered Phase 0 artifact
- `design/research_notes/Physics superhuman movement second pass/godot_runtime_verification.md`: the build, the test project and every runtime number quoted here
- `design/research_notes/Physics superhuman movement second pass/report_red_team_review.md`: the adversarial review whose ranked verdict and consolidated corrections this edition applies
- `design/research_notes/Physics superhuman movement second pass/*_pass2.md`: the thirteen verification notes (active ragdoll, biped control, destruction, first-person camera, first-person movement, Godot/Jolt implementation, Houdini pipeline, human biomechanics, learning to walk, Megaton Rainfall, learned character control, procedural animation, progression and gating), each with a verification table, gaps closed, new sources, contradictions, remaining gaps and better search terms
- `design/reports/Physics based superhuman movement design.md`: the first edition, superseded by this document
- `design/research_notes/Physics based superhuman movement design/`: the thirteen first-pass notes; where they disagree with the second-pass notes, the second-pass notes win
